# Second opinion (Phase 4.6), M6 T32: EmulationStation's game list views, scrapers and services, in pixelelated's EmulationStation fork

You are the one independent reviewer of a read-only renovation audit, read under a **new-functionality and brittleness lens**. Its subject is 42 files in three groups:
- `es-app/src/views/` and `views/gamelist/`: the system carousel and the six game-list styles;
- `es-app/src/scrapers/`: five metadata scrapers and the batch scrape's thread;
- `es-app/src/services/`: the local HTTP API on port 1234, over a vendored cpp-httplib.

They are read at the EmulationStation commit `16dbac5211`, which the distribution pins.
- **Six files are the fork's changes:** `Scraper.cpp`, `ThreadedScraper.cpp`/`.h`, `DetailedContainer.cpp`, `GridGameListView.cpp` and `ISimpleGameListView.cpp`. None was added by the fork.
- **Thirty-six are byte-identical to ROCKNIX's master.** They are read for brittleness. A defect in one is still closed when the fix is contained.

**What ships.** An immutable handheld OS that a person updates in place, keeping `/storage`.
- EmulationStation is its interface, at 640x480 on the smallest panel, run as root.
- The build defines `NDEBUG` (stated in `03` § 3.4), so rapidjson's assertions are compiled out.
- IGDB and ArcadeDB are built in every image. ScreenScraper is built under a runtime developer login. TheGamesDB and HfsDB only with their build macros.
- The HTTP API listens on loopback unless the player turns on WEB ACCESS.
- The batch scrape runs on a page the player sits in with CANCEL (`GuiScraperRun`, another slice's).
- The rules the code answers to are named in `03` § 3.2.

**What you get.**
- The first reader is another lab's model. You get its `02-forward-audit.md`, `03-retrospective.md` and `04-analysis.md` whole. `02`'s tables are generated by tools whose methods are stated in `02` beside them.
- Then context: the brief's questions and the register rows the analyses cite.
- Then the host harnesses' output (`stage-c/runs.txt`), the floor read (`stage-c/floor.md`), the include graph, and the guest window's records.
- Then **the six fork-modified files whole, line-numbered**.
- Then **line-numbered excerpts of the 36 upstream-carried files**, each cited range with eight lines of context. `httplib.h` is sent by its cited ranges only. The rest is trimmed (D-WORKFLOW-195), and the packet's manifest names every range sent.
- Then excerpts of the neighbours the leads turn on: `AsyncHandle.h`, `HttpReq`, `TimeUtil.cpp`, `ScraperSearchComponent.cpp`, `CollectionSystemManager.cpp`, `ViewController.cpp`, `FileData.cpp`, `FileSorts.h`, `GuiScraperRun.cpp`, `GuiMenu.cpp`, `Settings.cpp`.
- **When a claim needs lines outside the packet, say `outside the packet` and do not guess.**

**Masking.** Every section passed through a mask that replaces a value after `key=`, `key:`, `token=`, `user=`, `pass...=` or `psk=` with `***`. A `***` is the mask, not the code. The packet was scanned for credential shapes before it left; no hit was a value.

**Your job is to refute, narrow or confirm, against the code: not to agree.** Cite `file:line` for every claim, and say plainly where the first reader is wrong. Its line numbers are the pin's.

**What was run.** Nothing can be run for you.
- Seven host harnesses compiled the pin's own functions over doubles, under AddressSanitizer, against the recipe's rapidjson 1.1.0. Each failed at the pin and passed a constructed control (`stage-c/runs.txt`).
- Generators produced `02`'s tables. Each asserts its counts and was seen to fail on a constructed drift.
- A guest window ran an image whose EmulationStation is a later composition over the pin. It had no network, so no scraper's success path ran.

Answer these first, in this order:

1. **The scrape's outcome (3.13, 3.16, L-05, L-07).**
   - Is 3.13 right that an IO error, a spent 429 and a long Retry-After end `ASYNC_DONE`, so the page reads COMPLETED?
   - Would making them `setError(status, ...)` stop a batch run? Check it against `ThreadedScraper.cpp:155-173`'s fatal list.
   - Is anything else in `update()` a success path reached by failure?
   - Is 3.16's "a trap for 3.13's fix" right?
2. **The thread's lifetime (L-01, L-02, L-23).**
   - Is L-02's use-after-free reachable as the code stands, without the harness's hook?
   - Is the proposed fix (`stop()` under `sProgressMutex`, returning when `finished`; `mInstance` cleared under it) sufficient? `isRunning()` and `main.cpp`'s `stop()` at exit are also callers.
   - Is L-23's leak real?
3. **The parses (3.10, L-22, and the floor's 3.2, 3.4, 3.7, 3.8).**
   - Is any other rapidjson read in the slice unchecked in a way that is a wild read under `NDEBUG`? Name each `Get*` / `HasMember` / `operator[]` site.
4. **The interface thread and the bounds (L-06, L-14, L-18).**
   - Is L-18's read right that the token fetch runs on the interface thread at a batch run's start (`ThreadedScraper::start` -> `Process()` -> `ProcessNextGame` -> `ScraperThread::run`)?
   - Is the uncaught throw on the scrape thread reachable?
   - Which construction would a `stallTimeout` not cover?
5. **The services (L-12, L-13, L-19, L-20, L-21, L-24).**
   - Are the pool-thread claims right?
   - Is L-24's unbounded chunked body right at `httplib.h:2475` and `:2558-2559`?
   - Does anything else in the vendored httplib's request path make WEB ACCESS worse than unauthenticated control (a parse, a path traversal through a mount point, a header injection)?
6. **The views (3.27, 3.29, 3.31, 3.32, L-16, L-08, L-10, L-11).** Is any verdict wrong? Is L-16's free-then-read real as far as the packet shows?

Then list, as **new items**, any defect the first reader missed. Give each its `file:line`, its family, and what would prove it. The families are:
- a check whose failure reads as success;
- a call with no bound;
- a lifetime or a thread;
- a read of a file another process writes;
- a player sentence outside the vocabulary;
- a page the map does not describe.

**The shape of the answer.** One item per lead or finding, numbered `EP-1`, `EP-2`, ... Each item:
- names the lead (or `new`);
- gives a verdict: `confirmed`, `narrowed`, `refuted` or `new`;
- gives the evidence at `file:line`;
- says what the first reader should change, if anything.

Close with the items you would rank highest, and why.

The routing, every scraper but ScreenScraper: `ScraperHttpRequest::update` (`Scraper.cpp:289`) ends `ASYNC_DONE` with no error on a 404 or an IO error (`:372-376`, 3.13), retries a 429 (`:343-361`, `Retry-After` at `:351`; T21's `44d3c7e25` reads it through `ScraperReplies`, inherited), and calls `setError` on any other failure (`:638-640`, the body's words: L-05); `process()`'s return is discarded at `:337`. An error becomes the handle's status, `ThreadedScraper::processError` (`:155-175`) counts it in `errors` or, for its seven whole-run statuses (`:157`), sets `failed`, and the page's line 4 (T31's `GuiScraperRun`) shows `removeHtmlTags(getErrorMsg())`.

## The structure

- **The modes** (`UIModeController.{cpp,h}`): Full, Basic, Kid, Kiosk; the unlock sequence `listen()` `:55-97`, a wrong press resetting the counter at `:72` (3.33, settled); `UIMode = Full` and `saveFile()` at `:102-103` -- the right store for an ES-only mode. `SystemView.cpp:233`'s `UIMode` write has no `saveFile()` (L-11).
- **The registry and the build macros** (§ The scrapers): ScreenScraper under `SCREENSCRAPER_DEV_LOGIN` or `SCREENSCRAPER_RUNTIME_DEV_LOGIN` (#64), TheGamesDB under `GAMESDB_APIKEY`, HfsDB under `HFS_DEV_LOGIN`, IGDB and ArcadeDB always (D-QA-003; `device-builds.md` § Build credentials). The macro names only; no value is read.
- **The include cycle:** every `.cpp` under `views/` and `services/` includes `views/ViewController.h`, and `ViewController.cpp` includes the slice's headers back; taken as given (the brief, § 2). 47 include lines of the slice's 22 headers come from 23 files outside the 42 (`stage-a/callers.out`).
- **`httplib.h`'s currency, judged at stage C by content** (no version line; `Copyright (c) 2020` at `:4`). It carries `Client2` (`:1346`), `CPPHTTPLIB_DEPRECATED set_base_dir` beside `set_mount_point` (`:615-617`) and `set_idle_interval` (`:631`): the mid-2020 v0.7 series. ES's own history on it is three commits (08b4b7792 2020-06-04, 5733a3c03 2021-06-03, b4985a427 2021-11-08). The server is plain HTTP (`CPPHTTPLIB_OPENSSL_SUPPORT` is defined nowhere). It binds `127.0.0.1:1234`, or `0.0.0.0` when **WEB ACCESS** (`PublicWebAccess`, a player's switch at `GuiMenu.cpp:1088-1097`, default false at `Settings.cpp:190`) is on (`HttpServerThread.cpp:821-828`).
  - **Upstream check, 2026-10-27, one web search** (D-WORKFLOW-162; vulnerability databases and distribution trackers, not the upstream advisory itself):
    - CVE-2026-45372, an encoded CRLF in a percent-decoded header value, fixed in 0.44.0. The pin's server decodes only the path (`:3782`) and the query parameters (`:2796`), never a header value, so the mechanism as described is **absent here**.
    - CVE-2025-46728, no body-size limit under chunked transfer, fixed in 0.20.1. **Present here.** `CPPHTTPLIB_PAYLOAD_MAX_LENGTH` is `SIZE_MAX` (`:72`), ES never sets a limit, and `read_content_chunked` (`:2475`, `:2558-2559`) runs with no limit at all. A body grows until memory runs out. From the LAN only with WEB ACCESS on; from any local process always. L-24.

## The first reader's 03-retrospective.md (whole)

# Retrospective audit — M6.P2 T32, EmulationStation's game list views, scrapers and services (#679)

This was written by the first reader (Opus 5.5) after stages A and B, the guest window and the stage-C host proofs, and before any seat saw the packet. Line numbers are the pin's (`16dbac5211`). The leads are `02`'s: L-01 to L-21 from stages A and B, and L-22 to L-24 from stage C. The floor's 35 are `02` § The leads' second table.

## 3.1 Architectural coherence

- **Three subsystems share one directory tree and one thread, the interface thread.**
  - The views (`views/`, `views/gamelist/`) draw the carousel and the six list styles.
  - The scrapers (`scrapers/`) fetch metadata and media.
  - The services (`services/`) are the local HTTP API on port 1234.

  Each reaches the others through `ViewController` (T2's) and `FileData` (T21's and T26's). The include graph is 410 include lines in the 42, 143 of them into es-core, and 47 lines in from 23 files outside (`stage-c/includes.out`). Each `.cpp` under `views/` and `services/` includes `ViewController.h`, and `ViewController.cpp` includes the slice's headers back: the cycle the brief takes as given.
- **The scrapers have one request type and one reader.**
  - `ScraperHttpRequest::update` (`Scraper.cpp:289-385`) turns an `HttpReq` status into an `AsyncHandle` status.
  - `ThreadedScraper` (`:103-173`) reads it: a fatal list stops the run, any other error is counted, and DONE is a game with or without a match.

  The contract between them is a comment written in three scrapers (`IGDBScraper.cpp:313`, the GamesDB and ArcadeDB equivalents): "return false only when we reached a maximum scrap by minute, to retry". **No caller reads the return** (`Scraper.cpp:337`). IGDB returns false for "no match" (3.16). ScreenScraper, which ships in the fork's images, returns false for "maximum threads per minute reached" (`ScreenScraper.cpp:383-384`), so a rate-limited search reads as no match. An IO error and a spent 429 end as DONE (3.13). So the one place that turns a network failure into something the page can count drops it, and the page reads COMPLETED, GAMES SCRAPED: 9 offline (G-05). The page's count is itself short by the thread count, since `done` is set only at hand-out (3.19): G-05's guest had ten games.
- **The scrapers parse with rapidjson and check the shape after reading it.** Several sites call `HasMember` before `IsObject`, or `GetString` before `IsString`: 3.10 and 3.8 in GamesDB (its process, its images JSON and its resources), and L-22 in IGDB's and HfsDB's token reads. The recipe builds with NDEBUG, so rapidjson's assertion is compiled out, and a wrong shape is a wild read: two SEGVs at stage C.
- **The batch scrape is a fourth-tier page over a thread whose lifetime is its own.** `ThreadedScraper::run` ends with `delete this`, while `stop()` and `isRunning()` read the static `mInstance` under no lock. L-01 is the read; L-02's harness turns it into a use-after-free, and the same window marks a finished run cancelled. Each run also leaks its `ScraperThread`s and its `std::thread` (L-23).
- **The services run their handlers on httplib's pool.** Some post to the interface thread and some do not: `/notify` (L-12), `/removegames` and the media writes (L-13), `/launch`'s walk (L-19), and `/quit`, `/shutdown` and `/restart` (L-21). The vendored httplib is the 2020 v0.7 series with no body limit (L-24).
- **The views are upstream's, with the fork's additions in three files:**
  - `ISimpleGameListView.cpp`: #210's SAVE STATES (HOLD) help, the letter jump and the filter.
  - `GridGameListView.cpp`: #243's display aspect on the tiles.
  - `DetailedContainer.cpp`: the extras' aspect and the play-time row.

  `GameNameFormatter` is upstream's: its doubled ASCENDING and its `% 24` (3.32).

## 3.2 Project conformance

- **`engineering-practices.md` § Guards must fail closed, and blindspot 22.** `Scraper.cpp:372-376` turns an unreachable server into "no results" (3.13, G-05). The success path is reached by more than success: an IO error, a spent 429 and a long Retry-After all read as done.
- **`es-player-text.md` § Outcome vocabulary.**
  - The scrape page (T31's) can only say COMPLETED for a run that reached nothing, because the run hands it no error (3.13).
  - SKIPPED - YOU CANCELLED IT can be shown over a completed run (L-02).
  - Line 4 carries `removeHtmlTags(getErrorMsg())`, curl's or a server's words, for every scraper but ScreenScraper (L-05).
- **`es-code-traps.md` § A pooled connection with the link gone is silent for a quarter of an hour (#299).**
  - None of the nine `HttpReq` constructions in the slice sets `stallTimeout` or `timeout` (L-06, `t32-bounds.py`).
  - Two are on the stack and end in `wait()`, a 20 ms sleep loop (L-18).
  - L-18's read at stage C: the token fetch runs on the **interface thread** at a batch run's start and in three manual-scrape paths, so the bound on that freeze is the connect timeout plus nothing.
- **`es-native-ui.md` § Core patterns ("a file another process writes is read uncached").** All 49 `exists`-family reads in the 42 are cached (`t32-reads.py`). ES's own writers drop their entry, so the stale case is a writer outside the process. G-09 showed it on the image: a manual written from outside ES is absent until a restart (L-08).
- **`es-code-traps.md` § A rescan that deletes FileData drops the view first (#246).** The same free-then-read shape sits in `CollectionSystemManager::reloadCollection` behind the slice's filter (L-16, carried to T33). `POST /launch` posts raw `FileData*` from the pool thread (L-19, #774's sibling).
- **`es-player-text.md` § Every fork string ships in English and French.**
  - All 50 msgids in the slice have French (`t32-words.py`: 0 empty, 0 missing).
  - #210's SAVE STATES (HOLD) is translated with the game-save word (L-10).
  - `SystemView.cpp:233-234` shows an English dialog outside `_()` (L-11).
- **D-UI-039.** The view styles and the row that picks them are not in the menu map, and the views carry no screen title the checker can see (L-15).
- **D-UI-079 (change stays inside the lanes).** Of the stage-C proofs:
  - L-02 is in the fork's lane (the scrape page, D-UI-078).
  - 3.13 is upstream code under the fork's page.
  - 3.10, L-22, L-24, 3.27, 3.31 and 3.32 are upstream's.

  Each of these is a contained fix, so `bugs-are-agent-first.md` rule 6 applies. 3.27, 3.31 and 3.32 are views the fork does not work in: they are proposed as `bug`s only where the player sees them (3.31, 3.32), and recorded otherwise (3.27; G-04 shows no player-visible effect).

## 3.3 Spec fidelity

The brief's figures held at their hashes, with the corrections in `02` § The runs:
- `atol(` is 2 in 2;
- 25 path patterns over 27 registrations;
- 9 `HttpReq` constructions;
- the words 57 / 69 / 50, the brief having counted a comment;
- po L116 is the multi-line form, so its French is at L117.

Stage C adds three corrections:
- **3.32's second `atol` site does not wrap.** `secondsToString` folds the day back.
- **G-06's NO GAMES FIT is T31's PL-21 (#1254), not 3.16.** Corrected on #679 at 16:28 UTC.
- **L-16's `reloadCollection` then `reloadGameListView` is an either/or at `:461-464`.** The free-then-read is inside `reloadCollection`.

Of the six FAIL-at-pin leads, all six FAIL at the pin and pass their controls (`stage-c/runs.txt`). Three are stronger than the brief stated:
- 3.10 is a SEGV in the shipped build, not an assertion;
- 3.13 covers the spent 429 and the long Retry-After as well as the IO error;
- L-02 is a use-after-free.

The floor's other 27 are in `02` § The leads with their verdicts (`stage-c/floor.md`: 11 holds, 1 design, 15 findings; a read-only subagent's read, re-checked by the first reader where `floor.md` § Verification says). Two of the 15 change the picture: 3.15, a null dereference on SCRAPE NOW when the saved scraper is one this build does not register; and 3.19, which corrects G-05's 9 to ten games minus one thread.

## 3.4 Platform conformance

- **The image is M7's composition over the pin** (G-01: both literals). Every guest verdict is `image`.
- **The shipped theme has no grid view.** Grid falls back to the automatic view on the image, so L-14's per-tile read was not reached: 2.775 s against 2.786 s (G-02). 3.31's empty tile is therefore a theme-that-has-a-grid question, proved on the host only.
- **The build defines NDEBUG** (`config/functions`: the `ndebug` flag defaults on; ES's `PKG_BUILD_FLAGS` is `-gold`). This decides 3.10 and L-22: a crash, not an assertion.
- **The scrapers that ship:**
  - IGDB and ArcadeDB in every build.
  - ScreenScraper under the runtime developer login (#64, D-WORKFLOW-166).
  - TheGamesDB and HfsDB only with their macros.

  So 3.10 is reached only in a build with `GAMESDB_APIKEY`, while L-22's IGDB half and 3.16 are reached in every build by a player who set an IGDB client id.
- **The HTTP API is loopback-only by default.** WEB ACCESS opens it to the LAN, unauthenticated by upstream's design. L-24's unbounded body is reachable from the LAN then, and from any local process always (and ES runs as root).
- **A no-NIC guest reaches no scraper host.** G-07 and G-10 are `CANNOT`, with the reasons in `02` § The guest cycle; the success paths of the scrapers are therefore proved nowhere in this audit.

## 3.5 Cross-system interaction audit

### Interaction: `ScraperHttpRequest::update` x `ThreadedScraper`'s fatal list x T31's SCRAPING GAMES page

An IO error is DONE, so the reader counts no error and the page says COMPLETED (3.13, G-05). The fix must keep the 2020 intent that one game's failure does not stop a batch (a631e8c84). The reader already does that for a non-fatal code, so the fix is in the request, not the reader. Proved: `l313` (fixture), G-05 (image).

### Interaction: `stop()` x `run()`'s `delete this` x the page's `progress()`

The player's YES on CANCEL in the frame the last game ends gives two results (L-01, L-02):
- a write through a freed `ThreadedScraper`;
- `cancelled` over `finished`, so SKIPPED - YOU CANCELLED IT over a completed run.

Proved: `l02` (fixture, the window widened by a named hook).

### Interaction: a saved scraper name x a build's key set x SCRAPE NOW

The registry depends on the build's macros, while `Settings`' `Scraper` survives an upgrade. `GuiScraperStart` shows a fallback without saving it, and `ThreadedScraper::start` dereferences `getScraper()` (3.15). An image built with another key set, or an upgrade from one, crashes on SCRAPE NOW. `upgrade-and-install.md`'s case, tree; VM-provable by writing the setting on a guest.

### Interaction: IGDB's `ensureToken` x the interface thread x `generateRequests`' throw

The token fetch at a batch run's start runs on the interface thread, bounded by the connect timeout only (L-18). A later refetch on the scrape thread throws `INVALID CREDENTIALS` with no catch, and needs a 30-minute gap between searches (unproved). A malformed token body is a SEGV (L-22, `l316`).

### Interaction: the text filter x `CollectionSystemManager::reloadCollection` x `ViewController::reloadGameListView`

A grouped custom collection's root is cleared, then the bundle view's cursor is read (L-16). That is #246's shape, unproved here, and the holders are T33's.

### Interaction: `POST /launch` x the pool thread x a game-list reload

The walk over every system's files and a raw `FileData*` posted per match, with no `break`, against a reload on the interface thread (L-19). #774's sibling.

### Interaction: WEB ACCESS x httplib's unbounded body x ES as root

A LAN host can grow a request body until ES dies (L-24, tree).

## 3.6 Time to play

The slice is on the launch path at three points (01 § Read at stage C, item 10). What the window measured on the guest:
- **the populate on SCREENSHOTS:** 2.775 s from the press to a still screen in grid style (which the shipped theme draws as automatic), against 2.786 s automatic and 2.777 s for gb (G-02);
- **the list's press to `ViewController::launch`:** not timed (nothing was launched);
- **the carousel after UPDATE GAMELISTS:** framed, not timed (G-09).

The proposed fixes cost the launch path nothing: L-02's lock, 3.13's error status, the parses' shape checks, L-24's limit. L-18's fix moves a network wait **off** the interface thread at a scrape's start. No budget row exists for these numbers (`docs/vm-qa-log.md:9-18`).



## The first reader's 04-analysis.md (whole)

# Analysis — M6.P2 T32, EmulationStation's game list views, scrapers and services (#679)

Written by the first reader (Opus 5.5) at stage C, before the seat. Line numbers are the pin's (`16dbac5211`). The leads are `02` § The leads.

## Executive summary

The slice is 42 files and 17,311 lines. Six files carry the fork's changes and 36 are upstream's, byte for byte. It has three parts: the views, the scrapers and the local HTTP API. Each fails in the same way: **a failure that the next layer reads as success, or as nothing at all.**
- **The scrapers.**
  - A request that never reached its server ends as "done" (3.13). So an offline scrape says COMPLETED, GAMES SCRAPED: 9 (G-05, the image).
  - A response of the wrong shape is read before it is checked. In the shipped build, which has NDEBUG, that is a wild read: TheGamesDB's `"boxart": "x"` and IGDB's or HfsDB's numeric token are SEGVs (3.10, L-22).
- **The batch scrape's thread** frees itself while the player's CANCEL can still write through it. A CANCEL in the frame the run finishes is a use-after-free, and it marks the finished run cancelled (L-02).
- **The token fetches and media downloads** have no bound but the 10 s connect (L-06, L-18). The token fetch at a scrape's start runs on the interface thread.
- **The HTTP API** runs handlers that touch `FileData` on httplib's pool (L-12, L-13, L-19, L-21). Its 2020 httplib accepts an unbounded request body (L-24).
- **A saved scraper name the build does not register** crashes SCRAPE NOW (3.15). The page's count is short by the thread count (3.19).
- **The views are mostly sound.** Their defects are small and upstream's: a missing year (3.32), a wrapped play time (3.32), an empty tile in a grid theme (3.31), and a fast move whose wrong index a wrapping carousel hides (3.27).

All seven host harnesses FAIL at the pin and pass their controls. The window proved 3.13 and L-08 on the image. Proposed: **N bugs** (counted in `05`) and the rest Deferred or recorded, each named in `05`.

## Risk assessment

- **High, data or process safety:**
  - L-02, a use-after-free on the fork's own CANCEL path;
  - 3.10 and L-22, SEGVs on a malformed response. Each needs a server, a proxy or a captive portal to send the shape.
  - 3.15, a null dereference on SCRAPE NOW after an upgrade or a build change (tree; VM-provable).
- **Medium, a lie the player reads:**
  - 3.13, COMPLETED for a scrape that reached nothing;
  - L-02's SKIPPED over a completed run;
  - 3.19's count, short by the thread count;
  - L-05, curl's words on line 4;
  - L-08, a manual that appears only after a restart.
- **Medium, a freeze or a bound:**
  - L-18, the token fetch on the interface thread, bounded by the connect timeout;
  - L-06, a media download with no bound under a page whose only exit is CANCEL.
- **Low:**
  - L-24 (WEB ACCESS off by default);
  - 3.31 (no shipped theme has a grid view);
  - 3.32 (the year and the play time beside a name);
  - 3.27 (hidden by the wrap);
  - L-23 (a leak per run);
  - L-10 and L-11 (words).
- **Carried, not proved here:** L-16, #246's shape in `CollectionSystemManager` (T33's); L-19 (#774's sibling).

## Coverage boundary

**Read line by line:** the 42 paths at the pin, except `httplib.h`.
- `httplib.h` was read by fingerprint and at the request path's lines: `:72`, `:615-633`, `:2475`, `:2540-2575`, `:2735`, `:2796`, `:3782`. 02 § The structure gives the reason: a vendored library is checked for currency, not read whole.

**Read outside the 42, as context:**
- `ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:195-330` and `:636-670` (L-18);
- `CollectionSystemManager.cpp:1103-1140` and `:1541-1600` (L-16);
- `ViewController.cpp:1119-1150`;
- `FileData.cpp:2321-2327`;
- `TimeUtil.cpp:514-583` (3.32);
- `HttpReq.h`, `HttpReq.cpp:630-646`, `AsyncHandle.h`;
- `GuiMenu.cpp:1088-1097` and `Settings.cpp:190` (WEB ACCESS);
- the distribution's `config/functions:384-388` (NDEBUG) and `backuptool:1209-1218` (#274's strip).

**Not run:**
- any scraper's success path (G-07, G-10: `CANNOT`);
- L-14's per-tile cost (no grid view in the shipped theme);
- L-16's and L-19's reloads on a guest;
- a TSan run of L-01's unlocked reads.

## The questions

### Q1 Lifetimes and threads

- **L-01 and L-02.** `ThreadedScraper::stop()` (`:323-337`) reads `mInstance` and writes `thread->mExitCode` under no lock, while `run()` frees itself at `:238`. `l02` shows both halves at the pin:
  - a heap-use-after-free at `:333`;
  - `cancelled` over `finished` under `--plain`.

  The window was widened by a hook in the finishing block's `LOG`, and the harness says so. The fix shape (the control): `stop()` takes `sProgressMutex`, returns when `finished`, and `run()` clears `mInstance` under the same lock before `delete this`. `isRunning()` reads the same static and is with it. History: `stop()`'s CANCEL comment is the fork's (D-UI-078); the unlocked read is upstream's.
- **L-23.** `run()` erases each `ScraperThread` without a `delete` (`:218`, 717e7d8c3), and `mHandle` is never joined or freed (`:53`): a leak per run, upstream's.
- **L-12, L-13, L-19, L-21: the pool thread.**
  - `/notify` builds a toast off the interface thread (`HttpServerThread.cpp:574`).
  - `/removegames` and the media writes touch `FileData` there (`:738`, `:751-755`; `HttpApi.cpp:258-260`).
  - `/launch` walks every system's files there and posts one raw `FileData*` per match with no `break` (`:615-630`; #774's sibling, T2 PL-008).
  - `/quit`, `/shutdown` and `/restart` call `quitES` there (`:241`, `:249`, `:257`).

  Each is upstream's and contained. G-08 drove `/notify` and `/removegames` on the image without a crash, which proves no race either way.
- **L-16.** `ISimpleGameListView.cpp:461-464` is an either/or. The free-then-read is inside `CollectionSystemManager::reloadCollection` (`:1574-1589`): the root is cleared at `:1117`, then the bundle view's cursor is read (`ViewController.cpp:1140-1142`). #246's shape, carried to T33 with its lines.
- **The rescan callers that delete nothing** (`BasicGameListView.cpp:54`, `:182`, `CarouselGameListView.cpp:61`, `:171`) call `reloadGameListView(this)` with nothing freed first. Recorded sound.

### Q2 Bounded calls

- **L-06.** Nine `HttpReq` constructions; none sets `timeout` or `stallTimeout` (`t32-bounds.py`: `bounded=0`). Each ends at `HttpReq.cpp:204`'s 10 s connect and nothing else, so a link that goes after the connect is silent for `tcp_retries2` (#299's quarter of an hour). The scrape page's only exit is CANCEL, and CANCEL ends the loop, not the request in flight. The fix is #299's: a `stallTimeout` on the scrapers' options (`Scraper.cpp`'s `mOptions`, the media handle's, the two token requests').
- **L-18.**
  - The token requests are stack `HttpReq`s ended by `wait()`, a 20 ms sleep loop (`HttpReq.cpp:638-644`).
  - The batch run's first games are searched on the **interface thread** (`ThreadedScraper::start` -> `Process()` -> `ProcessNextGame` -> `ScraperThread::run` -> `search`, `:100`).
  - The manual scrape is on the interface thread where the player typed a name (`ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:303`), where no scraper takes the system (`:321`), and in `update()`'s retry (`:652`, `:658`).
  - The default manual path uses its own thread and catches the throw (`:206`, `:211-217`).
  - **Unproved:** on the scrape thread, `generateRequests`' `throw` (`IGDBScraper.cpp:172`, `HfsDBScraper.cpp:217`) has no catch. It needs a token older than 30 minutes, which needs a 30-minute gap between searches, and L-06 can make one.
- **L-24.** httplib's body limit is `SIZE_MAX` (`:72`), and chunked reads take no limit (`:2475`, `:2558-2559`). The fix is a contained `set_payload_max_length` where the server is built. The vendored copy's age is a #585 line (02 § The structure).
- **The cost the lens prices:** a token fetch on the interface thread is a freeze bounded only by the connect, at the moment the player presses SCRAPE NOW.

### Q3 Reads that read as success

- **3.13** (`l313`). `Scraper.cpp:372-376` makes `REQ_IO_ERROR` an "ignored error". `:350-352` makes a spent 429 DONE, and `:357-361` does the same for a Retry-After over 5 s where `retryOn249()` is false (IGDB and HfsDB in a manual scrape). The reader (`ThreadedScraper.cpp:155-173`) stops a run only on its fatal list, so `setError(status, ...)` for these keeps a631e8c84's intent (a batch survives one game) and gives the page a count. G-05 is the image's positive.
- **`:337` drops `process()`'s return.** The three scrapers' contract comment is therefore dead (3.5, 3.16). IGDB returns false for "no match" and on a parse error (`IGDBScraper.cpp:318-329`). A fix to 3.13 that honours the return would retry an empty answer as rate-limited, so 3.16's change lands with 3.13's or before it.
- **3.10, L-22.** The shape is read before it is checked, at `GamesDBJSONScraper.cpp:590`, `:598`, `:605-607`, `IGDBScraper.cpp:155-157` and `HfsDBScraper.cpp:202-204`. NDEBUG makes each a wild read. `l310` and `l316` show two SEGVs (rc -11 without the sanitizers). The fix is `IsObject` or `IsString` first (the controls). **The floor's parse items** (`stage-c/floor.md`): 3.2 and 3.4 hold; 3.7 drops a whole game on one bad boxart entry; 3.8 extends 3.10 to the images JSON (`:462-491`) and `GamesDBJSONScraperResources.cpp:180`.
- **ScreenScraper's rate limit** (`ScreenScraper.cpp:383-384`, outside the 42): "maximum threads per minute reached" returns `false`, which `:337` drops, so the game reads as no match. **So 3.13's fix should honour the return with a bounded retry, and 3.16 must be corrected with it.**
- **3.19.** `done` is set only as a game is handed out (`ThreadedScraper.cpp:250-256`, `:66`), so a finished run counts total minus threads: G-05's ten games read 9. A no-match is counted as scraped, against `ThreadedScraper.h:67`.
- **3.15.** `getScraper()` is dereferenced unchecked at `ThreadedScraper.cpp:296` and `:100`, and at `ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:321`. `GuiScraperStart.cpp:74-78` shows a fallback but saves only on change (`:215`).
- **L-07.** A media download's 404, IO error and spent 429 read as done (`Scraper.cpp:607-614`, `:631-635`): the caller half of #1191. #1205 (T21 PL-17) owns the HTTP-date `Retry-After` read as 0 ms.
- **L-02's cancel** is also this family: a CANCEL that did nothing reads as one that did.

### Q4 Uncached reads

- All 49 `exists`-family lines in the 42 are cached (`t32-reads.py`: `uncached=0`).
- ES's own writers drop their entry: `FileSystemUtil.cpp` `:1051`, `:1067`, `:1097`, `:1445`, `:1457-1458`, `:1506-1507`. So a stale answer needs a writer outside the process: the cloud content restore, a player over SSH, a scraper run by another tool.
- **L-08:** `DetailedContainer.cpp:903`, `:916` decide the MANUAL and MAP rows from such a read. G-09 is its positive with a control: the manual written from outside was absent in the session and present after a restart.
- **L-09:** the scrape decides what to fetch and what to overwrite from the same cache. A stream question: pass `false` for the reads whose writer is outside ES, as #179's rclone.conf gate did.

### Q5 The words, the French and the help bars

- **The strings** (`t32-words.py`): 57 code lines, 69 calls, 50 msgids, 0 with an empty French, 0 missing. The brief's 58 / 70 / 51 counted `ISimpleGameListView.cpp:905`'s comment.
- **L-10.** #210's `SAVE STATES (HOLD)` is `SAUVEGARDES (MAINTENU)` (po L218), the game-save word, where L233's `SAVE STATES` is `SAUVEGARDES D’ÉTAT`. One catalog line closes it.
- **L-11.** `SystemView.cpp:233-234`: an English sentence-case dialog outside `_()`, with a bare `"OK"`, after a `UIMode` write with no `saveFile()`. L116's empty `msgstr` is the multi-line form (its French at L117), so that half is withdrawn.
- **L-05.** Line 4 carries `removeHtmlTags(getErrorMsg())` (`Scraper.cpp:640`, `:638`'s `if`). G-05 reached no error, so line 4 stayed empty on the image, which is 3.13 seen from the page.
- **L-12.** G-08's long `/notify` body was clipped to one line at 640x480, against the one-line toast rule, which holds. The words are the caller's, unbounded, from the pool thread.
- **The help bars.** `getHelpPrompts()` is defined twice in the slice, and the five styles inherit `ISimpleGameListView`'s. **3.34** (#210's rule, `stage-c/floor.md`): SAVE STATES (HOLD) needs GameOptionsAtNorth and a `retroarch` game with a save-state config outside IMAGEVIEWER. The bar refreshes on a cursor change only in collections (`BasicGameListView.cpp:31`, `DetailedGameListView.cpp:39`). The French frame's five entries against six are consistent with a stale bar, not proved by it: an open question for a frame.
- **3.35** holds (`t32-words.py`; `stage-c/floor.md`). Only `": "` and `"- "` are concatenated outside `_()`, so French loses its ` : `.

### Q6 The views and their cost

| View | What draws it | What it reads per item on the interface thread | What the first frame waits for (guest) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Carousel (`SystemView`) | the systems' logos, `populate` (`:196`) | each system's game count and theme | framed after UPDATE GAMELISTS (G-09), not timed |
| Basic | a text list | the name (`GameNameFormatter`) | (G-02 frames) |
| Detailed | the list plus `DetailedContainer` | the image and extras' paths; MANUAL/MAP `exists` (L-08); the play time (3.32) | (G-02) |
| Video | Detailed plus a video | the video path | (G-02) |
| Grid | tiles | `DisplayAspect::forScreenshot` per tile (L-14, `GridGameListView.cpp:42-47`) | not reached: the shipped theme has no grid; 2.775 s press to still in grid style (drawn as automatic) |
| Carousel list | a game carousel | the image per item | (G-02) |

- **3.27** (`l327`): 25 of 312 cases out of range, all congruent modulo the size, so `moveSelectionBy`'s delta (`:407`, `:413`) on a wrapping carousel hides them. G-04 wrapped cleanly. The other 37 cases that are not congruent are the forward fast move on 3 to 9 systems: a no-op, by the `count >= sz` guard. Recorded, with a one-line contained fix.
- **3.31** (`l331`): IMAGE with no image draws an empty tile. The intent read is ambiguous (8f30c244c, 4371e3b7d), so it is a stream question.
- **3.32** (`l332`): no year under RELEASEDATE_DESCENDING, and `[01:00:00]` for 25 hours. The detailed row does not wrap.
- **3.29** (`floor.md`): a UTF-8 lead byte is taken as the letter, and the `break` at `:514` assumes name order. Neither explains G-03's cursor on Alpha under a name sort, so **G-03 is left open** for a frame in a stream.
- **3.30** is design (9be91629e). `BasicGameListView.cpp:101`'s `recurseFind` is a dead copy.
- **The #243 extras:** `DetailedContainer.cpp:1057-1072` and `ISimpleGameListView.cpp:755-765` reach both `mImage` and the theme's extras (`es-code-traps.md` § The picture beside a game list). Read, sound.
- **The cost.** The press to a still screen is 2.78 s in each style the theme draws (G-02), and no per-item read dominates it on the guest. L-14 remains a grid-theme question.

### Q7 The structure, the map and the merge debt

- **L-15.** `docs/es-menu-map.md` has no section for the five view styles, the rows that pick them (GAMELIST VIEW STYLE, T26's; VIEW OPTIONS, T31's) or the SCREENSHOTS list. The views carry no screen title, so `es-menu-map-check` cannot see them. `SystemView.cpp:552`'s computed title is #965's. The map section lands at stage E (checkbox 18).
- **The modes.** `UIModeController` has four modes. A wrong press resets the unlock counter (3.33, settled at `:72`), and `UIMode = Full` saves at `:102-103`. `SystemView.cpp:233`'s write does not save (L-11).
- **The registry** (`Scraper.cpp:24-39`): ScreenScraper under the runtime login, ArcadeDB and IGDB always, TheGamesDB and HfsDB with their macros. Names only. `IGDBSecret` is stripped from a settings backup by name (`backuptool:1218`; #274, D-QA-050). `IGDBClientID` is kept, an identifier not a secret.
- **`httplib.h`.** The 2020 v0.7 series (02 § The structure; L-24). A #585 line: the vendored copy is behind upstream's chunked-body limit (0.20.1). CVE-2026-45372's mechanism is absent.
- **The merge debt.** 0 of the 14 upstream commits since `cada856d86` touch the 42. The fork's six files carry 168/46. T21's ES tip carries `Scraper.cpp` 28/8 and `Scraper.h` 5/0, and adds `ScraperReplies.{cpp,h}`, inherited. The #585 candidates: #210's bar, the `Progress` contract, the aspect and the turn, `maskSecrets`, the `CheevosHash` argument. D-WORKFLOW-080's shape is recorded, not decided.

## Renovation face

### Q1 Modernize
The scrapers' parses want a shape-first helper, one function per response. The thread wants one owner of its lifetime. The services want every handler that touches `FileData` posted to the interface thread, as `/reloadgames` already does.

### Q2 Standards
rapidjson's preconditions are the standard here, and four sites break them (3.10, L-22, and the floor's). #299's stall bound is the standard for a request, and nine constructions lack it.

### Q3 Bugs
3.13, L-02, 3.10, L-22, L-06 with L-18, 3.32, 3.31, L-24. The proposed set and its owners are in `05`.

### Q4 Package currency
The vendored httplib (2020) is behind upstream (a #585 line). rapidjson is the recipe's 1.1.0, as everywhere in the tree.

### Q5 Efficiency
The token fetch on the interface thread. Nothing else measured on the guest dominates the 2.78 s.

### Q6 Dead ends
`process()`'s return (`:337`). The `count >= sz` guard's forward no-op (3.27). IMAGE's unreachable fallback (3.31).

### Q7 Clarity and structure
The contract comment repeated in three scrapers and read by no caller. `HttpServerThread.cpp`'s 27 registrations in one 650-line function (`:149-803`).

## Instruction File Recommendations

### Coverage gaps (would have prevented)
- `es-code-traps.md` has no line on rapidjson under NDEBUG: an unchecked `HasMember` or `GetString` is a wild read in the shipped build, not an assertion. 3.10 and L-22 are two instances.

### Codification gaps (proposals for the coordinator)
- **A shape-first parse rule.** "`IsObject`, `IsArray` or `IsString` before any member access on a body a network sent." A `grep` could audit it: `HasMember` or `GetString` with no `Is*` on the same value within N lines.

## Second opinion

(After the seat: the packet, the plan, the probe and the refutation, and the grades.)

### The floor's 35 leads, carried

The floor digest is in no tree, so its § 3 is here, one line each, in the
shape `file:line at the pin | the function | the defect a unit case would
catch`, with the brief's corrections applied (3.10 at `:607`, 3.16's first
half withdrawn, 3.26's range); **(fork-own)** marks lines the fork wrote.
Each is a unit case in `ScraperReplies`'s shape (§ 5 item 1) unless marked
guest-only; the six that FAIL at the pin are in bold.

## Context 2: the register rows

```text
| D-QA-003 | 2026-09-04 | **Fork builds stay without ScreenScraper and TheGamesDB; scraping waits.** ES compiles a scraper only when its credential is in the build environment (`SCREENSCRAPER_DEV_LOGIN`, `GAMESDB_APIKEY`, `HFS_DEV_LOGIN` — `CMakeLists.txt`, `Scraper.cpp`); IGDB and ArcadeDB remain, and IGDB wants the player's own Twitch client ID and secret. The build cost is one line in `~/.ROCKNIX/options`; the cost that matters is that a developer login is ScreenScraper's to grant, and upstream's belongs to upstream's CI. The journey's scrape step is exercised on an official image if and when it is needed. | maintainer, 2026-09-04 |

| D-UI-028 | 2026-09-10 | **Four outcome words for every cloud surface, then a why, what is in place, and how to recover.** `COMPLETED`; `COMPLETED WITH GAPS - <what>` (some of the run succeeded and some did not -- never reported as FAILED and never as COMPLETED, D-CLOUD-077); `COULDN'T FINISH - <why>` (nothing succeeded and it is not a sentinel); `SKIPPED - <reason>` only for the two sentinels (`NO NETWORK CONNECTION`, `ANOTHER CLOUD SYNC IS RUNNING`) and the launch cancel (`A GAME WAS STARTED`). The why comes from a `>>> why <sentence>` protocol line the scripts print at the failure point (rclone's taxonomy stays in the log), else from a small rc table; the in-place clause is one per verb (`WHAT WAS SENT IS IN YOUR CLOUD. THE REST IS STILL ON THIS DEVICE.`, `NOTHING ARRIVED. THIS DEVICE IS AS IT WAS.`); the recovery clause names the surface that retries (`TRY AGAIN`, or the row). `COMPLETED SUCCESSFULLY`, `SUCCEEDED`, bare `FAILED`, `STOPPED`, every log path and exit code retire from the screen. The card uses its action line for in-place + recovery; the transfer page keeps its seven fitted lines (D-UI-024) and gains `A TRY AGAIN  B CLOSE` on line 7 when the run did not complete. The row line under a toggle reads the same words with commas. Stamps gain a third field, the why token, additively. Register-of-record: the vocabulary table in `es-native-ui.md`. Fork resolved from the EmulationStation audit's proposal (docs/audits/2026_09_10-graceful-degradation). | #105 |

| D-UI-030 | 2026-09-10 | **A cloud run passes or fails; `COMPLETED WITH GAPS` is removed from the vocabulary (reverses that part of D-UI-028).** Maintainer, on getting it from a manual sync: *"if you don't know what the gaps are, it's not very helpful for you and it just makes you more anxious and trust the system less because it's working kind of. You'd rather just know it couldn't connect or it could connect."* A run whose parts disagree reads `COULDN'T FINISH - <why>` with the failing part's why, on card, page and rows alike. What did move is still said truthfully -- the card's in-place clause, the page's line 4 -- so nobody is told nothing happened when something did. The stamps keep the `gaps` token so a log can still tell a partial run from a total one. ES `cdbe284a5`. | #111 |

| D-UI-039 | 2026-09-11 | **Settings do not stack in one window: a group that grows past a few rows nests behind a row of its own, and `docs/es-menu-map.md` is updated in the same change as every row added, moved or renamed.** Maintainer: "I don't want to start stacking too many settings in one window, so we may need to think about nesting some of what we're doing. This is one reason we should make sure we're updating our mapped mermaid or D2 file of everything we've done in EmulationStation." The map had last been touched 2026-09-09 and lagged five row changes; brought current the same day. | #134, #23 |

| D-UI-078 | 2026-09-21 | **Long work runs on a foreground page that owns the screen until it ends; the only way out while it runs is CANCEL, which says what cancelling means for that job (start over, or resume where the job supports it); backgrounding is for fast work only.** Reverses the fourth-tier shape of D-UI-060 and D-UI-070 (#186 PL-07, #187, rule commit `0462cf0141`) for the offline-achievements scan, the cloud transfer page and the scraper; the other background cards (index, content installer, OS update, Bluetooth) are classified per surface in #241. Maintainer, on the RG35XX SP at the scan page's PRESS B TO KEEP SCANNING IN THE BACKGROUND: *"we should only allow things to run in the background when they're fast. If it's something that's going to take longer time, like scraping or doing the RetroAchievements offline work, we should force that to be on a foreground page."* And on the way out: *"We could add a cancel button if we want to give them the option of backing out. That way, they would know explicitly that they would have to start over or resume if we can support that. Backgrounding is tricky because there are more edge cases we're introducing for what has to happen in order for them to then finish it, surfacing an alert when it finishes, etc."* The background shape had no end signal: a scan left with B reported only on the row that launched it, which the player had to go and find. | #241, #236, #187, D-UI-060, D-UI-070, D-UI-028, D-CLOUD-129 |

| D-UI-079 | 2026-09-22 | **D-UI-078's foreground-page rule applies to the lanes the fork works in -- cloud sync and cloud behaviours, game saves, Wi-Fi, the offline achievements work -- and not to upstream surfaces outside them: the game index (`ThreadedHasher`), the content installer, the OS update and the Bluetooth scan keep their background cards for now.** Maintainer, 2026-09-22, closing #241's last box: *"We don't need to alter behaviors outside of the core work we've been tackling when it comes to the game index content installer, OS update, and Bluetooth scan. Let's not alter that work quite yet. I'm more comfortable modifying work within the different lanes we've been working in around cloud sync, cloud behaviors, game saves, Wi-Fi, etc."* The scraper was named in D-UI-078 itself and is done. | #241, D-UI-078 |

| D-WORKFLOW-162 | 2026-10-13 | **Every troubleshooting investigation checks upstream issue reports, release notes and fixes early.** Record the date, source links, affected/fixed versions and applicability; inspect the pinned source to establish whether a relevant fix is already included. A similar report is a lead, not a confirmed diagnosis; local reproduction and regression proof remain required. The maintainer requests this as standing instruction while investigating WebKitGTK memory pressure. | #573; #570; `.claude/rules/engineering-practices.md` |

| D-WORKFLOW-166 | 2026-10-14 | **Use the existing shared JELOS ScreenScraper developer identity as the interim choice for #566; evaluate replacement credentials separately in #586.** This refines D-QA-005/006's runtime-only policy: the intended release route provisions the selected shared developer identity so ordinary players need only their separate personal account. Dedicated credentials are not a prerequisite for the current candidate. Implementation and actual artifact/UI/backup/clean/upgrade checks remain due; this choice neither claims new provider authorization nor permits personal accounts, RA tokens or other unrelated private credentials in public images. D-INFRA-006/007 protections remain for those values; any exception must be limited to the named shared developer identity and verified by an executable check. | maintainer, 2026-10-14; #566; #586; `.claude/rules/device-builds.md` |

| D-WORKFLOW-195 | 2026-10-24 | **An Extended seat whose answer the model's output ceiling cuts on the packet is run again on a trimmed packet for that seat -- the same model, effort and provider; the analyses and tables whole, the cited files as line-numbered excerpts with context, the ask bounded -- never a model or effort substitution and never the same packet again.** `04` § Second opinion says the seats saw different evidence; an item one seat saw only in excerpt carries `excerpt`, and a disagreement that could be the excerpt's doing is resolved from the artifact, not from either seat. A fork resolved during execution on T30 (#677): Argon at `high` spent about 57k of its 65.5k output tokens reasoning on a 438 KB packet three times (`finish=length`), where T3's 308 KB packet took 30k. The maintainer may reverse it by a new row. | #677, #798; `adversarial-council.md` § Council model floor; the T30 hold post of 10:04 UTC and the coordinator's answer the same hour |

| D-UI-129 | 2026-10-27 | **`SKIPPED - <reason>` admits a player's own cancel of a run they started.** D-UI-028/030 set the sentinels at rclone's 69 and 75 and the launch cancel; the scrape page's CANCEL (`SKIPPED - YOU CANCELLED IT`, `ThreadedScraper.cpp` at the pin, D-UI-078's page) is the same kind of fact as a game started over a sync: nothing went wrong, the player chose, so `COULDN'T FINISH` would say what is not true. The set is now 69, 75, the launch cancel, and a player's own cancel on a fourth-tier page (`es-player-text.md` § Outcome vocabulary takes the fourth entry). A completed run reported as cancelled (`stop()` after `run()` finished, T32's lead L-02) is a defect under this row, not a word to reword. | #678 (PL-13); D-UI-028; D-UI-030; D-UI-078 |
```

```text
# T32 (#679) stage C: the floor's 27 items read at their lines

The floor's items marked "at C" in `02` § The leads: 27 ids (the pointer said 28; 3.26 is the guest's, 3.33 settled, and the six FAIL-at-pin leads are `stage-c/runs.txt`'s). Read at the pin (`git show 16dbac5211:`) by a read-only subagent under the first reader's brief, then checked by the first reader as listed in § Verification. Its verdicts: 11 holds, 1 design, 15 findings.

## Verification by the first reader

- **Re-read and confirmed at their lines:**
  - 3.15. `Scraper::getScraper` (`Scraper.cpp:67-78`) returns `nullptr` for an unregistered name. `ThreadedScraper.cpp:296` dereferences it. `GuiScraperStart.cpp:74-78` shows the first entry, and Settings is written only by the change callback (`:215`), so a saved name the build does not register reaches SCRAPE NOW unchanged. `isValidConfiguredScraper` is called only by `ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:384`.
  - 3.19, with G-05 as its positive on the image. `sProgress.done` is set only in `updateUI` (`:250-256`), called as a game is handed out (`:66`). G-05's guest had **ten** gb games (`guest-cycle/scripts/seed.sh:7`) and read **GAMES SCRAPED: 9** under ArcadeDB's one thread: total minus the thread count. 02's G-05 row is corrected to say so.
  - 3.5. Every exit returns `true`, and `:337` drops the return, consistent with `l313`.
  - 3.28. Every grouping loop steps by one, consistent with `l327`.
  - 3.8's `:590`, `:598` and `:605-607`, which `l310` drives (two SEGV-class shapes at the pin).
  - The ArcadeDB precedence slip at `ArcadeDBJSONScraper.cpp:56`.
  - ScreenScraper's "maximum threads per minute reached" returning `false` (`ScreenScraper.cpp:383-384`, outside the 42). The return is dropped at `Scraper.cpp:337`, so a rate-limited ScreenScraper search reads as no match. ScreenScraper ships in the fork's images (#64).
- **Carried as the subagent's read, not re-read by the first reader:** 3.3, 3.7, 3.11, 3.12, 3.17, 3.18, 3.20, 3.22, 3.23, 3.24, 3.29, 3.30, 3.34, and the out-of-scope list below except the two above. They go to the seat with that label.
- **The subagent did not read the build's NDEBUG.** The first reader did (`config/functions:384-388`; `03` § 3.4), so its "release behaviour unproved" is settled for 3.8: a wild read, as `l310` shows.

| id | verdict | evidence (file:line, short quote or input → result) | history read (hash or "none") |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 3.1 | holds | ArcadeDBJSONScraper.cpp:34-37/:42-45 strip `" "` and `"."`, then `HttpReq::urlEncode` (es-core HttpReq.cpp:25-45) keeps only `A-Za-z0-9-_.~`, so it percent-encodes every other byte. `&`, `#` and `?` become `%26 %23 %3F`, and UTF-8 `É` becomes `%C3%89`. Nothing escapes the `game_name=` value. | none |
| 3.2 | holds | `getStringOrThrow`/`getIntOrThrow` (:76-101) check `HasMember` and `IsString`/`IsInt` before `Get*`, and every optional field (:134-158) checks its type first. Silent defaults: `players` given as a string such as `"2"` is dropped (:157). `Rating` is always `"-1"` (:160). A missing `title` throws, the catch at :236 logs it, and that game is skipped. | none |
| 3.3 | finding (low) | `getMediaTagNames` (:103-118) hard-codes `url_image_ingame/_title/_flyer/_marquee/url_video_shortplay`. If upstream renames a key, `findMedia` (:121-127) returns `""` and no log line is written, so the game is scraped with text and no media. A `ScrapperImageSrc` outside the list (e.g. `fanart`) gives the same silent empty result. | none |
| 3.4 | holds | `findMedia` (:123-125) walks the tags in list order. Video tries `url_video_shortplay` before `_hd` (:116). `HasMember && IsString` precede `GetString`, so a non-string value is skipped and never read. | none |
| 3.5 | holds | Every exit returns `true`: parse error (:210, with `setError`), wrong release (:217, no setError, so it reads as "no match"), no result array (:224), and normal (:242). The comment at :199 is stale. Scraper.cpp:337 discards `process()`'s return, and 3a997b651 removed the retry-on-false. | 3a997b651 2019-10-25 Fabrice CARUSO "Scraper : Better management for 404 & 429 errors"; 71c098d72 |
| 3.6 | holds | `isSupportedPlatform` (:183-193) returns false with no log when none of a system's platforms is in the map (:27-168), so that system is not offered TheGamesDB. :252 warns per unmapped platform only for mixed systems. The ids are hand-typed and will drift. `TANDY` shares `4941` with `TRS80_COLOR_COMPUTER` (:91-92); whether that is correct is unproved. The scraper is compiled only with `GAMESDB_APIKEY`. | none |
| 3.7 | finding | `getBoxartImage` (:293-310) calls `getStringOrThrow` on `type` and `side` for every element. `{"type":"boxart","side":null,...}` throws at :303, and an `[0]` without `filename` throws at :309. process() catches `std::runtime_error` (:624), so the game's whole result is dropped (name, description and media), not only its boxart. :493 shows that `side` can be non-string. | none |
| 3.8 | finding | Missing shape checks. :590/:558 call `doc.HasMember` without `doc.IsObject()`. :598 calls `doc["include"].HasMember` unchecked. :607 tests `boxart.IsObject()` *after* two `HasMember`s, and :415 reads `boxart["base_url"]` unchecked. The images JSON (:462-491) never checks the parse error or `IsObject`, and calls `GetString` on `medium`/`large`/`type`/`filename` without `IsString`. Resources.cpp:180 does the same. Debug builds abort; release behaviour is unproved and needs a harness. | none |
| 3.9 | holds | `preProcess` (:563-572) skips any game whose `id` is not an int (:566). processGame then throws on `getIntOrThrow(game,"id")` (:445), so that game is dropped in both places, consistently. See the out-of-scope notes for what a failed dependency does. | 71ff744d8 2025-12-07 (dependency requests) |
| 3.11 | finding (low) | A typed tag matches only metadata `name == tagName+"type"` (:385), else `description == tagType` (:390-394), else `continue`. If the server's field names differ, `cover2d/back` (box-2D-back, :338) has no untyped fallback, so BoxBack is never scraped and nothing is logged. `ss` falls through to bare `screenshot` (:311), so the first screenshot is used, possibly the title shot. | b4985a427 |
| 3.12 | finding | The arcade filter (:410-423) drops a result whose `system.id` equals any mapped platform other than ARCADE/NEOGEO/MODEL3. `TEKNOPARROT` was mapped to `"29794"` (:29, 886eb1909) and never excluded, so on any ARCADE system every result with `system.id 29794` is dropped. `process` (:621-622) then `break`s after the first game even if it was filtered, so an arcade description query yields nothing. Compiled only with `HFS_DEV_LOGIN`. | 886eb1909 2023-10-15 "[Platform] Add teknoparrot platform"; b4985a427 |
| 3.14 | holds | `ScraperSearchHandle::update` (:212-256) moves to the next request only when the current one is `ASYNC_DONE` and `mResults` is empty (:246). It stops at the first request that produced results (:240-243). An `ASYNC_ERROR` on any request empties the queue and fails the search (:224-233), so later URLs are not tried. | none |
| 3.15 | finding | `getScraper` (:67-78) returns nullptr for a name that is not registered. ThreadedScraper.cpp:296 (`->getThreadCount`), :100 (`->search`) and ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:321 dereference it unchecked. Input: Settings `Scraper=TheGamesDB` or `HfsDB` on a build without that key. GuiScraperStart.cpp:74-78 shows the first entry but never writes Settings, so SCRAPE NOW dereferences null. Unproved on a device: needs a harness. | none |
| 3.17 | finding | The table is duplicated at Scraper.cpp:653-667 and HttpApi.cpp:213-227 (identical today). `Content-Type: image/png; charset=binary` gives `.png; charset=binary`, and the file is renamed to that (:669-676). A 2xx `text/html` body keeps the `.png` name; FreeImage falls back to the filename (:709), Load returns NULL, width 0 → true, and :458 records the HTML as the game's image. | none |
| 3.18 | finding | `getSaveAsPath` (:784-799) has no case for `Image` or `Mix` (or other non-media ids). `Mix` falls through to `images/<stem>-image<ext>`, the Image's own name. `POST /systems/x/games/<id>/media/mix` with `image/png` (HttpApi.cpp:233-259; Mix is MD_PATH) overwrites `-image.png` and points both fields at it. The editor cannot reach this (Mix is `isStatistic`, MetaData.cpp:66). | none |
| 3.19 | finding | `done` is set only in `updateUI`, which runs when a game is handed out (:66, :250). The last `threadCount` games finish with no update, so the final count is `total - threads` (1 game, 1 thread → `GAMES SCRAPED: 0` and the UPDATE GAMELISTS note hidden, GuiScraperRun.cpp:194/231). A no-match is `ASYNC_DONE` with no error (:125-129), so it is counted as scraped, against the comment at ThreadedScraper.h:67. | 7dc8bf373 (fork page) not read further |
| 3.20 | finding | A thread leaves IN_PROGRESS only when its handle does (:105, :135). Scraper requests use the default `HttpReqOptions` (`timeout=0`, `stallTimeout=0`, HttpReq.h:53-54), so a stalled pooled connection keeps the thread IN_PROGRESS for TCP's retry span (es-code-traps #299). A 429 with a large `Retry-After` and `retryOn249()` waits that long (Scraper.cpp:351-367). CANCEL exits. The duration is unproved: needs a harness. | none |
| 3.21 | holds | The game id is `md5(game->getPath())` (:102-108) and systems are addressed by name, so ids stay stable across a reload while the path does not change. A rename or move changes the id. `findFileData` (:110-130) recomputes the md5 of every game on every call (O(n)). | none |
| 3.22 | finding | Non-string values are skipped silently (:192-193). `{"favorite":true}` or `{"rating":0.8}` changes nothing, returns false, and HttpServerThread.cpp:476-487 answers `404 game not found` for a game that exists. A no-op POST of the current values also gets 404. MD_PATH keys are ignored (:183). | none |
| 3.23 | finding | `image/svg+xml` maps to `.svg` (:218-219). The image whitelist (:253) is `.jpg/.png/.gif` only, so it returns false. The caller replies `404 media not found` (HttpServerThread.cpp:451-452), with no reason and the wrong status. The same happens to `image/webp` and `application/octet-stream`. | none |
| 3.24 | finding | `jpeg`/`jpg` map to the non-standard `image/jpg` (:99-100). There are no entries for `mp4/webm/mkv/avi/mov/cbz/bmp`, so `GET .../media/video` is served as `text/plain` (:124, :393). Whether browsers then refuse to play it is unproved: needs a harness. `ext[0]` on an empty ext is safe in C++11. | none |
| 3.25 | holds | The server binds `127.0.0.1`, or `0.0.0.0` when PublicWebAccess is on (:823-828). Both are IPv4 only, so `::1` cannot connect at all and never reaches `isAllowed` (:129). When the server is not public, every peer is therefore `127.0.0.1`. Toggling PublicWebAccess at runtime changes `isAllowed` but not the bind until a restart. | none |
| 3.28 | holds | `_moveCursorInRange` (:272-280) steps ±1 modulo sz, so every grouping loop (:295, :302, :317, :324) returns to `mCursor` within sz steps. Indices stay in [0,sz) and `cursor<size` is checked at :287/:309. The default branch can return a negative value (sz=5, cursor 4, backward → -1); callers use it only as a delta (:407, :413). | 1d9212bec, 474dde4c6 |
| 3.29 | finding | `toupper(name[0])` (:511, :554, :603) takes a UTF-8 lead byte as the letter: `Élite` and `Ãngel` both file under `"\xC3"`, which the JUMP TO LETTER list shows as invalid UTF-8. On x86 (signed char) this is UB. The `break` at :514 assumes name order: sorted by rating as `[Zelda, Mario, Metroid, Zork, Mega]`, "M" lands on Mega (index 4), not Mario. | none |
| 3.30 | design | When nothing matches, `findChildrenListToDisplayAtCursor` (FileData.cpp:1699-1723, the live copy) reaches `stack.empty()` and returns the root's items. The list is repopulated at the root, the folder stack is cleared, and :145 still speaks the unseen name. This was introduced by 9be91629e for filter changes. BasicGameListView.cpp:101 `recurseFind` is a dead copy. | 9be91629e 2021-04-07 "Fix list reload after filtering changed when selected game is in a subfolder." |
| 3.34 | finding | SAVE STATES (HOLD) is shown only when GameOptionsAtNorth is on (:711-714) and the cursor is a non-null GAME (:364) whose emulator is `retroarch`, whose system is not IMAGEVIEWER, and whose system has ≥1 save-state config (SaveStateRepository.cpp:211-227). Kid mode, panel proportion and popup state do not affect it. Defect: the bar is refreshed on cursor change only in collections (BasicGameListView.cpp:31, DetailedGameListView.cpp:39), so a regular list's prompt can be stale. | 7dc8bf373 2026-09-18 (#210); 474125e0d; 8266ba1ea |
| 3.35 | holds | Every label word goes through `_()` (:112-140, :1010, :1043), and the folder text through `ngettext` (:627-629). Only punctuation is outside: the `": "` suffix is concatenated in code, so French cannot get its ` : `, and `"- "` bullets are added at :617. :1447 is commented out. | none |

## Defects noticed outside the 27 items

- ArcadeDBJSONScraper.cpp:56: `system && A || B || C` binds as `(system && A) || B || C`, so a null `system` is dereferenced.
- ArcadeDBJSONScraper.cpp:213-218: a `release` below 4 does not call `setError`, so a format change reads as "no match", not an error.
- ScreenScraper.cpp:384-385 with Scraper.cpp:337: "maximum threads per minute reached" returns false to ask for a retry, but the return value has been discarded since 3a997b651. The game is reported as no match.
- Scraper.cpp:372-383: these branches also apply to dependency requests (TheGamesDB `/Games/Images`). A 404 or IO error on one game's images request sets DONE without `process()`, which discards every result of the search. A 4xx/5xx fails the whole search.
- Scraper.cpp:596: a 429 retry rebuilds the request with `HttpReq(url, mSavePath)`, which drops `options.customHeaders` (the ScreenScraper `Referer`).
- Scraper.cpp:556: `maxheight` is set from `maxWidth` (both axes use the width). This may be an intentional square bound; unproved.
- Scraper.cpp:495-501: one failed media download fails the whole resolve, so the game's text metadata is discarded too. The `ResolvePair`s are deleted but left in `mFuncs`, dangling.
- HttpApi.cpp:258: the result of `writeAllText` is not checked; metadata is set and success returned even when the write failed.
- HttpServerThread.cpp:390-397: when a media file cannot be read, `GET .../media/<type>` returns 200 with an empty body instead of 404.
- ThreadedScraper.cpp:220: an erased `ScraperThread*` is never deleted (leak). ThreadedScraper.cpp:335 writes `mExitCode` from the UI thread without atomics, and `mPaused` (:181) is the same; these are data races.
- HfsDBScraper.cpp:204: `doc["token"].GetString()` is called without `IsString`/`IsObject`. :192 posts the user and password without URL-encoding them. :222 refreshes `mTokenDate` on every request, so the 30-minute local expiry (:172) never fires during a run.
- GuiScraperStart.cpp:74-78: the fallback scraper selection is not written to Settings, which is the path to the 3.15 null dereference. ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:321 also dereferences null.
- SystemView.cpp:274: the guard checks only `count >= sz`. With fewer than 10 systems, fast-forward does nothing while fast-backward moves -10 mod sz. SystemView.cpp:284: the local `mCursor` shadows the member.
- BasicGameListView.cpp:101: `recurseFind` is dead code with external linkage, superseded by `FolderData::findChildrenListToDisplayAtCursor`.
- GamesDBJSONScraper.cpp:92: `TANDY` and `TRS80_COLOR_COMPUTER` share id 4941. That this is wrong is unproved without the TheGamesDB platform list.
```

  206  // ScraperSearchHandle
  207  ScraperSearchHandle::ScraperSearchHandle()
  208  {
  209  	setStatus(ASYNC_IN_PROGRESS);
  210  }
  211  
  212  void ScraperSearchHandle::update()
  213  {
  214  	if(mStatus == ASYNC_DONE)
  215  		return;
  216  
  217  	if(!mRequestQueue.empty())
  218  	{
  219  		// a request can add more requests to the queue while running,
  220  		// so be careful with references into the queue
  221  		auto& req = *(mRequestQueue.front());
  222  		AsyncHandleStatus status = req.status();
  223  
  224  		if(status == ASYNC_ERROR)
  225  		{
  226  			// propagate error
  227  			setError(req.getErrorCode(), Utils::String::removeHtmlTags(req.getStatusString()));
  228  
  229  			// empty our queue
  230  			while(!mRequestQueue.empty())
  231  				mRequestQueue.pop();
  232  
  233  			return;
  234  		}
  235  
  236  		// finished this one, see if we have any more
  237  		if (status == ASYNC_DONE)
  238  		{
  239  			// If we have results, exit, else process the next request
  240  			if (mResults.size() > 0)
  241  			{
  242  				while (!mRequestQueue.empty())
  243  					mRequestQueue.pop();
  244  			}
  245  			else
  246  				mRequestQueue.pop();
  247  		}
  248  	}
  249  
  250  	// we finished without any errors!
  251  	if(mRequestQueue.empty() && mStatus != ASYNC_ERROR)
  252  	{
  253  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE);
  254  		return;
  255  	}
  256  }
  257  
  258  // ScraperHttpRequest
  259  ScraperHttpRequest::ScraperHttpRequest(std::vector<ScraperSearchResult>& resultsWrite, const std::string& url, HttpReqOptions* options)
  260  	: ScraperRequest(resultsWrite)
  261  {
  262  	setStatus(ASYNC_IN_PROGRESS);
  263  
  264  	if (options != nullptr)
  265  		mOptions = *options;
  266  	
  267  	mRequest = new HttpReq(url, &mOptions);
  268  	mUrl = url;
  269  	mRetryCount = 0;
  270  	mOverQuotaPendingTime = 0;
  271  	mOverQuotaRetryDelay = OVERQUOTA_RETRY_DELAY;
  272  	mOverQuotaRetryCount = OVERQUOTA_RETRY_COUNT;
  273  }
  274  
  275  ScraperHttpRequest::~ScraperHttpRequest()
  276  {
  277  	delete mRequest;	
  278  }
  279  
  280  std::string ScraperHttpRequest::getDependencyResponse(const std::string& id) const
  281  {
  282  	auto it = mResponses.find(id);
  283  	if (it == mResponses.cend())
  284  		return "";
  285  
  286  	return it->second;
  287  }
  288  
  289  void ScraperHttpRequest::update()
  290  {
  291  	if (mOverQuotaPendingTime > 0)
  292  	{
  293  		int lastTime = SDL_GetTicks();
  294  		if (lastTime - mOverQuotaPendingTime > mOverQuotaRetryDelay)
  295  		{
  296  			mOverQuotaPendingTime = 0;
  297  
  298  			LOG(LogDebug) << "REQ_429_TOOMANYREQUESTS : Retrying";
  299  
  300  			std::string url = mRequest->getUrl();
  301  			delete mRequest;
  302  			mRequest = new HttpReq(url, &mOptions);
  303  		}
  304  
  305  		return;
  306  	}
  307  
  308  	HttpReq::Status status = mRequest->status();
  309  
  310  	// not ready yet
  311  	if (status == HttpReq::REQ_IN_PROGRESS)
  312  		return;
  313  
  314  	if (status == HttpReq::REQ_SUCCESS)
  315  	{
  316  		std::string body = mRequest->getContent();
  317  		mResponses[mDependencyId] = body;
  318  
  319  		if (mDependencyId.empty())
  320  			preProcess(body);
  321  
  322  		if (!mDependencyQueue.empty())
  323  		{
  324  			status = HttpReq::REQ_IN_PROGRESS;
  325  				
  326  			auto item = mDependencyQueue.front();
  327  			mDependencyQueue.pop();
  328  
  329  			mDependencyId = item.first;
  330  			std::string url = item.second;	
  331  			delete mRequest;
  332  			mRequest = new HttpReq(url, &mOptions);
  333  			return;
  334  		}
  335  
  336  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE); // if process() has an error, status will be changed to ASYNC_ERROR
  337  		process(mResponses[""], mResults);
  338  		return;
  339  	}
  340  
  341  	if (status == HttpReq::REQ_429_TOOMANYREQUESTS)
  342  	{
  343  		mRetryCount++;
  344  		if (mRetryCount >= mOverQuotaRetryCount)
  345  		{
  346  			setStatus(ASYNC_DONE); // Ignore error
  347  			return;
  348  		}
  349  
  350  
  351  		auto retryDelay = mRequest->getResponseHeader("Retry-After");
  352  		if (!retryDelay.empty())
  353  		{
  354  			mOverQuotaRetryCount = 1;
  355  			mOverQuotaRetryDelay = Utils::String::toInteger(retryDelay) * 1000;
  356  
  357  			if (!retryOn249() && mOverQuotaRetryDelay > 5000)
  358  			{
  359  				setStatus(ASYNC_DONE); // Ignore error if delay > 5 seconds
  360  				return;
  361  			}
  362  		}
  363  
  364  		setStatus(ASYNC_IN_PROGRESS);
  365  
  366  		mOverQuotaPendingTime = SDL_GetTicks();
  367  		LOG(LogDebug) << "REQ_429_TOOMANYREQUESTS : Retrying in " << mOverQuotaRetryDelay << " seconds";
  368  		return;
  369  	}
  370  
  371  	// Ignored errors
  372  	if (status == HttpReq::REQ_404_NOTFOUND || status == HttpReq::REQ_IO_ERROR)
  373  	{
  374  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE);
  375  		return;
  376  	}
  377  
  378  	// Blocking errors
  379  	if (status != HttpReq::REQ_SUCCESS)
  380  	{		
  381  		setError(status, Utils::String::removeHtmlTags(mRequest->getErrorMsg()));
  382  		return;
  383  	}	
  384  
  385  	// everything else is some sort of error
  386  	LOG(LogError) << "ScraperHttpRequest network error (status: " << status << ") - " << mRequest->getErrorMsg();
  387  	setError(Utils::String::removeHtmlTags(mRequest->getErrorMsg()));
  388  }

  390  std::unique_ptr<MDResolveHandle> ScraperSearchResult::resolveMetaDataAssets(const ScraperSearchParams& search)
  391  {
  392  	return std::unique_ptr<MDResolveHandle>(new MDResolveHandle(*this, search));
  393  }
  394  
  395  // metadata resolving stuff
  396  MDResolveHandle::MDResolveHandle(const ScraperSearchResult& result, const ScraperSearchParams& search) : mResult(result)
  397  {
  398  	mPercent = -1;
  399  
  400  	bool overWriteMedias = Settings::getInstance()->getBool("ScrapeOverWrite") && search.overWriteMedias;
  401  
  402  	for (auto& url : result.urls)
  403  	{
  404  		if (url.second.url.empty())
  405  			continue;
  406  
  407  		if (!overWriteMedias && Utils::FileSystem::exists(search.game->getMetadata(url.first)))
  408  		{
  409  			mResult.mdl.set(url.first, search.game->getMetadata(url.first));
  410  			if (mResult.urls.find(url.first) != mResult.urls.cend())
  411  				mResult.urls[url.first].url = "";
  412  
  413  			continue;
  414  		}
  415  
  416  		bool resize = true;
  417  
  418  		std::string suffix = "image";
  419  		switch (url.first)
  420  		{
  421  		case MetaDataId::Video: suffix = "video";  resize = false; break;
  422  		case MetaDataId::FanArt: suffix = "fanart"; resize = false; break;
  423  		case MetaDataId::BoxBack: suffix = "boxback"; resize = false; break;
  424  		case MetaDataId::BoxArt: suffix = "box"; resize = false; break;
  425  		case MetaDataId::Wheel: suffix = "wheel"; resize = false; break;		
  426  		case MetaDataId::TitleShot: suffix = "titleshot"; break;
  427  		case MetaDataId::Manual: suffix = "manual"; resize = false;  break;
  428  		case MetaDataId::Magazine: suffix = "magazine"; resize = false;  break;
  429  		case MetaDataId::Map: suffix = "map"; resize = false; break;
  430  		case MetaDataId::Cartridge: suffix = "cartridge"; break;
  431  		case MetaDataId::Bezel: suffix = "bezel"; resize = false; break;
  432  		}
  433  
  434  		auto ext = url.second.format;
  435  		if (ext.empty())
  436  			ext = Utils::FileSystem::getExtension(url.second.url);
  437  
  438  		std::string resourcePath = Scraper::getSaveAsPath(search.game, url.first, ext);
  439  
  440  		if (!overWriteMedias && Utils::FileSystem::exists(resourcePath))
  441  		{
  442  			mResult.mdl.set(url.first, resourcePath);
  443  			if (mResult.urls.find(url.first) != mResult.urls.cend())
  444  				mResult.urls[url.first].url = "";
  445  
  446  			continue;
  447  		}
  448  
  449  		mFuncs.push_back(new ResolvePair(
  450  			[this, url, resourcePath, resize] 
  451  			{ 
  452  				return downloadImageAsync(url.second.url, resourcePath, resize); 
  453  			},
  454  			[this, url](ImageDownloadHandle* result)
  455  			{
  456  				auto finalFile = result->getImageFileName();
  457  
  458  				if (Utils::FileSystem::getFileSize(finalFile) > 0)
  459  					mResult.mdl.set(url.first, finalFile);
  460  
  461  				if (mResult.urls.find(url.first) != mResult.urls.cend())
  462  					mResult.urls[url.first].url = "";
  463  			},
  464  			suffix, result.mdl.getName()));
  465  	}
  466  
  467  	auto it = mFuncs.cbegin();
  468  	if (it == mFuncs.cend())
  469  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE);
  470  	else
  471  	{
  472  		mSource = (*it)->source;
  473  		mCurrentItem = (*it)->name;
  474  		(*it)->Run();		
  475  	}
  476  }
  477  
  478  void MDResolveHandle::update()
  479  {
  480  	if(mStatus == ASYNC_DONE || mStatus == ASYNC_ERROR)
  481  		return;
  482  	
  483  	auto it = mFuncs.cbegin();
  484  	if (it == mFuncs.cend())
  485  	{
  486  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE);
  487  		return;
  488  	}
  489  
  490  	ResolvePair* pPair = (*it);
  491  		
  492  	if (pPair->handle->status() == ASYNC_IN_PROGRESS)
  493  		mPercent = pPair->handle->getPercent();
  494  
  495  	if (pPair->handle->status() == ASYNC_ERROR)
  496  	{
  497  		setError(pPair->handle->getErrorCode(), pPair->handle->getStatusString());
  498  		for (auto fc : mFuncs)
  499  			delete fc;
  500  
  501  		return;
  502  	}
  503  	else if (pPair->handle->status() == ASYNC_DONE)
  504  	{		
  505  		pPair->onFinished(pPair->handle.get());
  506  		mFuncs.erase(it);
  507  		delete pPair;
  508  
  509  		auto next = mFuncs.cbegin();
  510  		if (next != mFuncs.cend())
  511  		{
  512  			mSource = (*next)->source;
  513  			mCurrentItem = (*next)->name;
  514  			(*next)->Run();
  515  		}
  516  	}
  517  	
  518  	if(mFuncs.empty())
  519  		setStatus(ASYNC_DONE);
  520  }
